有限纠正v2.1修复验证

四道选定回归题，各运行一次。原问题、原始草稿和首次证据相同；只运行修复后的审校及有限补查。没有调用Jev/Luna，不计算或更新32题总分。

以下判断为Codex按实际来源和运行记录检查，不能代替独立评测。原有32题报告保持不变。

PFI2-014 按照作者的解释，PFI打浆为什么会提高WRV，又如何与纤维间结合联系起来？

目标结构层级问题已修正，同时补入FTIR相对含量与作者推测限定。

修复前

PFI打浆的主要作用包括内部细纤维化（纤维壁分层、疏松和润胀）、外部细纤维化、纤维切断和细小组分形成，并提高纤维的可及性、润胀、柔性和纤维间结合能力。 [3]
作者将打浆度和WRV随PFI转数增加而显著增加归因于更高程度的细纤维化产生了新的表面和更多孔隙。 [2]
WRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向更柔顺，从而有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [2]
作者推测，O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键的减少可能源于PFI机械作用下纤维素分子链之间的断裂，并与内部和外部细纤维化密切相关。 [1]
较少的分子间氢键和较低的结晶指数会通过提高分子链运动性、可及性和WRV来进一步提高纤维素纤维的柔性。 [1] [4]

修复后

PFI打浆的主要作用包括内部细纤维化（纤维壁分层、疏松和润胀）、外部细纤维化、纤维切断和细小组分形成，并提高纤维的可及性、润胀、柔性和纤维间结合能力。 [3]
作者将打浆度和WRV随PFI转数增加而显著增加归因于更高程度的细纤维化产生了新的表面和更多孔隙。 [2]
WRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向更柔顺，从而有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [2]
FT-IR反卷积得到的O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键相对含量在PFI打浆后总体下降，作者推测这可能源于PFI机械作用下纤维素分子链之间的断裂，并与内部和外部细纤维化密切相关。 [1]
作者指出，较少的分子间氢键和较低的结晶指数会进一步提高纤维素纤维的柔性，原因涉及较高的分子链运动性以及可及性和WRV。 [1] [4]

Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.",

          "page_end": 10,
          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

      "ref": "[3]",
      "chunk_id": "chk_c51ca42969d4d4498a37",

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",

Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

      "ref": "[3]",
      "chunk_id": "chk_c51ca42969d4d4498a37",
      "document_id": "doc_0fcf2a50cfa02d21",
      "filename": "Li-2020-PFI-refining-aggregation-structure-cellulose.pdf",
      "page_start": 1,
      "page_end": 2,
      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.",

PFI2-023 针叶木的O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键含量和CI-XI的Pearson相关系数是多少？

目标引用缺失已修正：冲突句同时引用正文[1]与摘要[2]，同一陈述两来源检查正常通过。

修复前

正文3.4报告，针叶木（softwood fibers）的O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数为0.912（p<0.05）。 [1]
该文摘要则报告针叶木的同一Pearson相关系数为0.888（p<0.05），与正文3.4的0.912不一致。 [2]

修复后

正文3.4报告，针叶木（softwood fibers）的O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数为0.912（p<0.05）。 [1]
该文摘要则报告针叶木的同一Pearson相关系数为0.888（p<0.05），与正文3.4的0.912不一致。 [1] [2]

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nBecause the recorded infrared spectrum is the convolution of the real spectrum and the linear function of instrument. The band of hydroxyl groups $( 3 0 0 0 { - } 3 7 0 0 \\mathrm { c m } ^ { - 1 } )$ was deconvoluted into four predominant bands in the present study based on the peak positions above. Subsequently, the relative content of various H-bonds was calculated based on area proportion of the band, respectively. Finally, the data and curves obtained using nonlinear fitting for softwood fibers is shown in Table 2a and Figure 7, and the results of hardwood fibers can be seen in Table 2b and Figure S3.\n\n[原文片段不连续]\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. The deconvoluted FT-IR spectra of softwood fibers at various revolutions: (a) 0 r, (b) 4000 r, (c) 8000 r, (d) 12,000 r, and (e) 16,000 r.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. It decreased by 7.72% from 28.23% to 26.05% as refining from 0r to 4000 r and then increased by 9.14% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by a decrease of 17.13%, with the revolutions increasing from 8000 r to 16,000 r. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nA mixture of inter-molecular and intra-molecular H-bonds is considered to be the cause for the broadening of OH bands in the FT-IR spectra. Results and Discussions > 3.2. Efect of PFI Refining on Crystalline Index and Crystallite Size\n[原文]\nThe trends of CI (CI-XI and CI-XA) for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers were all similar, decreasing first and then increasing, along with a decline, as shown in Figure 4. The fluctuation of the CI was probably because of the mechanical action of PFI refining on the diferent regions of cellulose. In the first refining stage, the decrease of the CI showed that mechanical treatment may mainly act in the crystalline region. Subsequently, the growth of the CI could be interpreted as greater force acting on the amorphous region in which cocrystallization might occur upon drying under the mechanical and thermal action of PFI refining. The subsequent decrease might be the continuous destruction of crystalline region.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.2.       "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",
      "score": 0.6670678853988647,
      "source_chunks": [
        {
          "chunk_id": "chk_fe625e14c33a8b51f13a",
          "chunk_index": 4,
          "page_start": 1,
          "page_end": 1,
          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",
          "kind": "paragraph"
        }

Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nA mixture of inter-molecular and intra-molecular H-bonds is considered to be the cause for the broadening of OH bands in the FT-IR spectra. Therefore, taking derivative of the OH bands can improve the resolution of various H-bonds. Normally, $3 5 8 0 { - } 3 5 5 0 \\mathrm { c m } ^ { - 1 }$ is for free OH(2) and $\\mathrm { O H } ( 6 ) , 3 4 5 5 { - } 3 4 1 0 \\mathrm { c m } ^ { - 1 }$ ic stacking of adjacent microfiis for O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds, $3 3 7 5 { - } 3 3 4 0 \\mathrm { c m } ^ { - 1 }$ theis for $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ nes were alignedintra-molecular H-bonds, and $3 3 1 0 { - } 3 2 3 0 \\ \\mathrm { c m } ^ { - 1 }$ is for $\\mathrm { O } ( 6 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 3 ^ { \\prime } )$ inter-molecular H-bonds in cellulose I [31,32].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nThe peak position assigned to $\\mathrm { O } ( 6 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 3 ^ { \\prime } )$ inter-molecular H-bonds (around $3 2 2 7 ~ \\mathrm { c m } ^ { - 1 } )$ O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds (around 3346 cm<sup>−1</sup>), and O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds (around 3452 cm<sup>−1</sup>) of softwood fibers and hardwood fibers almost kept constant in the whole refining process, as shown in Figure 6a,b. According to the formulae of H-bonding energy and H-bonding distance (Equations (4) and (5)), the H-bonding energies and H-bonding distances of the three H-bonds remained unchanged, as the peak positions of three H-bonds almost did not exhibit red shift or blue shift. The results showed that PFI refining did not change the H-bonding energies and H-bonding distances, just as it did not change the cellulose polymorph in the above results\n\n[原文片段不连续]\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.2. Efect of PFI Refining on Crystalline Index and Crystallite Size\n[原文]\nThe trends of CI (CI-XI and CI-XA) for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers were all similar, decreasing first and then increasing, along with a decline, as shown in Figure 4. The fluctuation of the CI was probably because of the mechanical action of PFI refining on the diferent regions of cellulose. In the first refining stage, the decrease of the CI showed that mechanical treatment may mainly act in the crystalline region. Subsequently, the growth of the CI could be interpreted as greater force acting on the amorphous region in which cocrystallization might occur upon drying under the mechanical and thermal action of PFI refining. The subsequent decrease might be the continuous destruction of crystalline region.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.2. PFI2-024 PFI打浆是怎么一步步让纸变结实的？纤维长度、细小纤维、WRV这几个因素里哪个贡献最大？

仍未完成：审校耗尽32768 Token输出上限，原回答保留，未伪装为修订成功。

修复前

PFI打浆对纤维的主要作用包括内部细纤维化（细胞壁分层、松弛和润胀）、外部细纤维化（表面微细纤维剥离但仍附着）、纤维切断和细小纤维生成。 [2]
这些作用会提高纤维的可及性、润胀性、柔顺性和纤维间结合能力，从而为成纸强度提高提供基础。 [2]
随PFI转数增加，打浆度和WRV显著升高；文中归因于细纤维化产生新的表面和更多孔隙。 [1]
WRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向更柔顺，这有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]
Figure 1显示，PFI打浆后软木浆的纤维长度下降，而细小纤维含量上升，同时宏观细纤维化指数上升。 [1]
PFI打浆提高了细纤维化程度（尤其是内部细纤维化）、纤维润胀和纸页拉伸强度，且零-span结果说明未降低单根纤维强度。 [1]
分子层面，O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键总量在打浆中下降，且更少的分子间氢键和较低结晶指数有利于提高纤维柔顺性、可及性和WRV。 [3]
湿纸幅强度方面，软木浆的增加比硬木浆更显著，作者强调纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起重要作用。 [1]
所给证据没有给出纤维长度、细小纤维和WRV三者对成纸强度贡献的定量排序；从直接论述看，WRV/润胀（内部细纤维化）与纤维间结合强度的联系最直接，而纤维长度主要被强调为湿纸幅强度的关键因素。 [1] [2]

修复后

PFI打浆对纤维的主要作用包括内部细纤维化（细胞壁分层、松弛和润胀）、外部细纤维化（表面微细纤维剥离但仍附着）、纤维切断和细小纤维生成。 [2]
这些作用会提高纤维的可及性、润胀性、柔顺性和纤维间结合能力，从而为成纸强度提高提供基础。 [2]
随PFI转数增加，打浆度和WRV显著升高；文中归因于细纤维化产生新的表面和更多孔隙。 [1]
WRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向更柔顺，这有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]
Figure 1显示，PFI打浆后软木浆的纤维长度下降，而细小纤维含量上升，同时宏观细纤维化指数上升。 [1]
PFI打浆提高了细纤维化程度（尤其是内部细纤维化）、纤维润胀和纸页拉伸强度，且零-span结果说明未降低单根纤维强度。 [1]
分子层面，O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键总量在打浆中下降，且更少的分子间氢键和较低结晶指数有利于提高纤维柔顺性、可及性和WRV。 [3]
湿纸幅强度方面，软木浆的增加比硬木浆更显著，作者强调纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起重要作用。 [1]

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",
      "score": 0.7951879501342773,

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.",
          "kind": "paragraph"
        },

          "chunk_id": "chk_150883badd0c9b531207",
          "chunk_index": 7,
          "page_start": 1,
          "page_end": 1,
          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",

Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nFor the content of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the trend can be <sup>For</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>content</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>inter-molecular</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup> <sup>softwood</sup> <sup>fiber,</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>trend</sup> <sup>can</sup> <sup>be</sup>divided into two stages, as shown in Table 2a. Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

    "answer": "PFI打浆对纤维的主要作用包括内部细纤维化（细胞壁分层、松弛和润胀）、外部细纤维化（表面微细纤维剥离但仍附着）、纤维切断和细小纤维生成。 [2]\n这些作用会提高纤维的可及性、润胀性、柔顺性和纤维间结合能力，从而为成纸强度提高提供基础。 [2]\n随PFI转数增加，打浆度和WRV显著升高；文中归因于细纤维化产生新的表面和更多孔隙。 [1]\nWRV表征的润胀增加使纤维在横向更柔顺，这有助于提高纤维网络的纤维间结合强度。 [1]\nFigure 1显示，PFI打浆后软木浆的纤维长度下降，而细小纤维含量上升，同时宏观细纤维化指数上升。 [1]\nPFI打浆提高了细纤维化程度（尤其是内部细纤维化）、纤维润胀和纸页拉伸强度，且零-span结果说明未降低单根纤维强度。 [1]\n分子层面，O(6)H···O(3′)分子间氢键总量在打浆中下降，且更少的分子间氢键和较低结晶指数有利于提高纤维柔顺性、可及性和WRV。 [3]\n湿纸幅强度方面，软木浆的增加比硬木浆更显著，作者强调纤维长度对湿纸幅强度起重要作用。 [1]\n所给证据没有给出纤维长度、细小纤维和WRV三者对成纸强度贡献的定量排序；从直接论述看，WRV/润胀（内部细纤维化）与纤维间结合强度的联系最直接，而纤维长度主要被强调为湿纸幅强度的关键因素。 [1] [2]",

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nMechanical, chemical, and biological treatments are usually adopted to obtain the desired physical and mechanical properties and for further utilization of the natural cellulosic fibers. Therein, Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining is the most common laboratory refiner in order to improve the inter-fiber bonding capacity [1] and accessibility [2,3], and manufacture the micro-fibrillated or nano-fibrillated cellulose [4–6]. The main efects of PFI refining on fibers are: (i) internal fibrillation—delaminating, loosening, and swelling of fiber wall; (ii) external fibrillation—peeling of fibrils from the fiber surface while remaining attached; (iii) fiber shortening—fiber cutting due to shearing action, and (iv) fines formation—primary fines come from ray cells and parenchyma cells and microfibrils that are caused by fiber shortening or external fibrillation. Moreover, the PFI refining could be adopted to obtain a higher internal fibrillation than other laboratory refiners [1]. These efects of the PFI refining on cellulosic fibers will improve the accessibility, the swelling, the flexibility, and the inter-fiber bonding capacity of fibers.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\n It is assumed that these changes in the macro-level structure and properties are related to the changes of crystallinity and H-bonding network during refining [7,8].\n\nThere are some diferent conclusions regarding to the efects of PFI refining on crystallinity of cellulose, not like that of ball milling, which efectively reduces the crystallinity as the treatment progresses. Although some researchers considered that the crystallinity decreased during PFI refining for corn stover [9] or softwood pulp [10], some researchers reported that the crystallinity increased first and then decreased with the degree of refining for hardwood bleached kraft pulp fibers [11] or for unbleached eucalyptus fibers [12]. Moreover, Zhao et al. [7] reported that the crystallinity initially fluctuated upward and then downward, followed by a repeating fluctuation with PFI refining for tobacco stem mechanical pulp. None of the previous studies identified a clear trend, but the results were that the crystallinity varied during PFI refinement. Based on the large proportion of H-bonds in crystalline region of cellulose, it can be assumed that the H-bonding network might change during PFI refining.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nFourier transformation infrared spectroscopy (FT-IR), which is sensitive to molecular structure, can be as an indirect measurement for studying H-bonding network. FT-IR deconvolution of hydroxyl groups band was adopted in order to study the changes of the energy of H-bonds and the H-bonding distance during delignification [13] or the deconstruction of cellulose [14]. Moreover, it was used to study the diferences of the content of H-bonds not only between dislocation region and normal region [15], but also among plant species [16,17]. Regarding the changes of H-bonding network during PFI refining, Yuan et al. [18] reported that the content of H-bonds and crystallinity index (CI) of eucalyptus fibers changed with the increment of beating time. Subsequently, Zhao et al. [8] found that the H-bonding energy for O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) inter-molecular H-bonds decreased by 12.9% and evolutions of the content of certain H-bonds difered at various refining stages.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 1. Introduction\n[原文]\nThere is still a lack of literature on the efects of PFI refinement on the H-bonding network. Particularly, the relationship between the content of H-bonds and crystallinity is still worthy of further study, which will deepen the understanding of the molecular aggregation and distribution of H-bonds, providing a molecular-scale explanation for the variations in accessibility, swelling, flexibility, and fibrillation of cellulosic fibers during PFI refinement. Therefore, this work explored the efects of PFI refinement on the CI, crystallite size, energy of H-bonds, H-bonding distance, and content of H-bonds for softwood pulp fibers and hardwood pulp fibers. Attempts were made in order to establish the correlations between the CI and content of various H-bonds.",

Regarding the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds of <sup>with</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>revolutions</sup> <sup>increasing</sup> <sup>from</sup> <sup>8000</sup> <sup>r</sup> <sup>to</sup> <sup>16,000</sup> <sup>r.</sup> <sup>Regarding</sup> <sup>the</sup> <sup>O(6)H O(3′)</sup> <sup>H-bonds</sup> <sup>of</sup>hardwood fiber, the trend was almost similar to that of softwood fibers, except for a small increase of 0.45% from 8000 r to 12,000 r (in Table 2b).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\n On the whole, the amount of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers decreased by 16.54% and 5.91% altogether, respectively. The decrease might be due to the cleavage among cellulose molecular chains under the mechanical action of PFI refining, which was closely related to internal and external fibrillation (as seen in Section 3.1). On the other hand, the increase illustrates that there was not only the breakage, but also the formation of O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds. Both the fewer inter-molecular H-bonds and the lower CI (as Section 3.2 shows) will further improve the flexibility of cellulosic fibers, due to the higher mobility of molecular chains, the accessibility, and the WRV of fibers (as seen in Section 3.1).\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the O(3)H· · · O(5) intra-molecular H-bonds of softwood fiber, the content had a growth of 10.09% from 28.44% to 31.31% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r, and then had a slight decrease of 1.44% from 4000 r to 8000 r, followed by an increase of 13.16% until the end of refining. Overall, the trend was similar to that of hardwood fibers, which first increased and then had a decrease, followed by a growth. During the whole refining, the amount of $\\mathrm { O } ( 3 ) \\mathrm { H } \\cdots \\mathrm { O } ( 5 )$ H-bonds for softwood fibers and hardwood fibers showed an upward tendency and increased by 22.78% and 8.8%, respectively. The increase might be because the stable O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds might have not only less breakage under the action of water as destructor to the crystallites [30], but also creation in the amorphous region upon drying. Because hydrogen bond–covalent bond synergies play an important role in the cellulose stifness axial modulus, more O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds will enhance the cellulose stifness [33,34].\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nIn the case of the O(2)H· · · O(6) intra-molecular H-bonds for softwood fiber, the trend of content can be described as two phases. It had an increase of 3.5% from 31.4% to 32.5% in the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r and it then decreased by 21.57% from 4000 r to 12000 r, along with a growth of 15.26% in the last refining stage. For the content of the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds of hardwood fiber, it increased firstly and then had a decrease that was also followed by an increase. For both fibers, the content of O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds decreased, except for the stage of refining from 0 r to 4000 r. It might be due to the fact that the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds, which are much more accessible to water, are less stable than O(3)H· · · O(5) H-bonds.\n\n[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 3. Results and Discussions > 3.3. Efect of PFI Refining on H-Bonding Energies, H-Bonding Distances, and Content of H-Bonds\n[原文]\nAs to the free OH(2) and OH(6) of softwood fiber, the relative content showed a slight increase trend in general. The enhancement of the free OH(2) and OH(6) was understandable, because there was the breakage of H-bonds among cellulosic molecules, which resulted in more extensive delamination and higher specific surface area (as shown in Figure S4 and Table S1 in Online Resource). This made cellulose more accessible to water and chemicals. However, for the free OH(2) and OH(6) of hardwood fiber, the number of the free OH(2) and OH(6) decreased by 21.65% from 14.32% to 11.22%, with revolutions increasing from 0 r to 4000 r, followed by an increase of 45.37% in the stage of refining from 4000 r to 12,000 r, along with an decrease of 17.11%. It could be explained by the instability of free OH groups, which makes it easy to form new H-bonds, such as O(2)H· · · O(6) or O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>)H-bonds. Thus although there is the formation of free OH(2) and OH(6) during fibrillation, the content of the free OH(2) and OH(6) might disease; meanwhile, the content of the O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) or the O(2)H· · · O(6) H-bonds might increase upon drying.",

This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

PFI2-032 论文里氢键含量和CI-XI的相关系数是针叶木0.919、阔叶木0.925吧？这么高是不是就能用CI-XI直接算出氢键含量了？

目标漏答已修正：补入同篇摘要[7]，第二轮写明摘要0.888与正文0.912不一致，并保留相关不等于直接换算。

修复前

0.919和0.925不是氢键含量与CI-XI的相关系数，而是CI-XI与CI-XA的相关系数：针叶木0.919、阔叶木0.925（p < 0.05）。 [1]
由FT-IR峰面积比例得到的O(6)H···O(3′)氢键相对含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数为针叶木0.912、阔叶木0.889（p < 0.05）。 [2] [6]
作者结论仅指出CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量显著正相关，认为在PFI精磨过程中可基于CI-XI大致推测该氢键含量的趋势。 [4]
根据提供的这些结果与结论（PFI精磨过程中的针叶木/阔叶木样品），作者只报告了CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量显著正相关，并认为可基于CI-XI大致推测该氢键含量的趋势；这些结果与结论中未提出由CI-XI直接计算氢键含量的公式或定量换算关系，相关不等于可直接计算。 [2] [4]
并非所有氢键都与CI-XI强相关：O(2)H···O(6)或游离OH与CI-XI的相关性较差，O(3)H···O(5)与CI-XI的相关性未在文中讨论。 [2]

修复后

0.919和0.925不是氢键含量与CI-XI的相关系数，而是CI-XI与CI-XA的相关系数：针叶木0.919、阔叶木0.925（p < 0.05）。 [1]
O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数在结果部分为针叶木0.912、阔叶木0.889（p < 0.05），但摘要报告针叶木0.888、阔叶木0.889（p < 0.05），两处针叶木数值不一致。 [2] [7]
作者结论仅指出CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量显著正相关，认为在PFI精磨过程中可基于CI-XI大致推测该氢键含量的趋势。 [4]
所给结果和结论只报告了CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量的相关性，并称可据此大致推测该氢键含量的趋势；不能把相关系数当作由CI-XI直接计算或定量换算氢键含量的依据。 [2] [4]
并非所有氢键都与CI-XI强相关：O(2)H···O(6)或游离OH与CI-XI的相关性较差，O(3)H···O(5)与CI-XI的相关性未在文中讨论。 [2]
来源检查与限制

        "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

            "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

    "answer": "0.919和0.925不是氢键含量与CI-XI的相关系数，而是CI-XI与CI-XA的相关系数：针叶木0.919、阔叶木0.925（p < 0.05）。 [1]\nO(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量与CI-XI的Pearson相关系数在结果部分为针叶木0.912、阔叶木0.889（p < 0.05），但摘要报告针叶木0.888、阔叶木0.889（p < 0.05），两处针叶木数值不一致。 [2] [7]\n作者结论仅指出CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量显著正相关，认为在PFI精磨过程中可基于CI-XI大致推测该氢键含量的趋势。 [4]\n所给结果和结论只报告了CI-XI与O(6)H···O(3′)氢键含量的相关性，并称可据此大致推测该氢键含量的趋势；不能把相关系数当作由CI-XI直接计算或定量换算氢键含量的依据。 [2] [4]\n并非所有氢键都与CI-XI强相关：O(2)H···O(6)或游离OH与CI-XI的相关性较差，O(3)H···O(5)与CI-XI的相关性未在文中讨论。 [2]",

      "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",

          "text": "[章节标签（程序整理，非原文）] 未确定\n[原文]\nAbstract: Supramolecular structure is the critical factor that afects the properties of cellulosic fibers. This article studied the action of Papirindustriens forskningsinstitut (PFI) refining on the molecular aggregation and hydrogen bonding network, and tried to explore the relationship between the crystal packing and hydrogen-bonding network in cellulosic fibers. The results showed that the polymorph, H-bonding distance, and H-bonding energy of various H-bonds remained almost unchanged, while the crystalline index, crystallite size, and content of various H-bonds changed with refining. Therein, the content of the inter-molecular O(6)H· · · O(3<sup>0</sup>) H-bonds was significantly correlated with the crystalline index that was obtained in intensities of the XRD peaks. The Pearson correlation coeficient between them was 0.888 (p < 0.05) for softwood fibers and 0.889 (p < 0.05) for hardwood fibers, respectively. It can be concluded that the variations of accessibility, swelling, and fibrillation were closely related to the supramolecular structure and the intermolecular H-bonds play an important role in the crystal packing of cellulose.",
